# Roslyn MCP multi-session retrospective — 2026-09-13 — 14-day window — Claude Code + Codex

## 0. Sources and coverage

**Window** 2026-08-26T12:55:00Z → 2026-09-09T12:55:00Z (14 days). **Report** `C:/Code-Repo/Roslyn-Backed-MCP/ai_docs/reports/20260913T200750Z_roslyn-backed-mcp_roslyn-mcp-multisession-retro.md`. **Included** 187 sessions: claude 17, codex 170. Both sources were readable (`sources_degraded: false`). Coverage is incomplete, so `truncated: true`; see **Truncation and known omissions**.

**Spec exception (window rule).** The spec selects sessions by file mtime. This report does not: sessions were prefiltered by mtime and then kept only when their record timestamps fall inside both window bounds. Claude Code session files on this machine were bulk-rewritten without adding timestamped records, so mtime stopped tracking activity. The deviation is stated in full under **Window rule**, and its cost — 2 qualifying Codex sessions that were never extracted — is carried as truncation rows 1–2 and is absent from every count in this report.

**Frontmatter values this section fixes:** `truncated: true`, `codex_subagent_sessions_rolled_up: 177`.

- **Quotes:** any quote containing `\"` or `\n` is raw JSONL bytes.
- **Counts:** counts come from the extraction unless marked *re-derived*, which means they were recomputed from the transcripts in this pass. **Counting bases across sections** gives the basis for each count and lists the ones that disagree with other sections.
- **Every Codex count in this report is static call-site occurrences, not resolved invocations** — including the §2a per-tool rows. See method caveat 2.

- **Filter rules:**
  - Claude call: a `tool_use` under either Roslyn prefix, in the session or its subagent transcripts.
  - Codex call: a static `tools.mcp__roslyn__<tool>(` occurrence in an exec input, in the parent or a child rollout. The rule misses handle and bracket dispatch (method caveat 2), so it dropped sessions that did make real calls.
  - Text-only triage kept only substantive discussion of Roslyn MCP use. It rejected process-cleanup mentions, template boilerplate, automated security-review diffs, edits to this repo's own source, and the retro prompt itself.
- **Removed by the miner:** claude f7528674 (zero Roslyn calls; design work on this server itself) and one Codex duplicate placeholder row (01a03e6a) that was not a real session.
- **Substantive discoverability-only sessions:** one each in bioremote, dotnet-network-documentation (01a04322, a counting miss; see method caveats) and snipcue. The spec's relevance filter includes substantive discussion, so all 3 should have been included. They are listed under truncation.

**Window rule (deviation from the spec)**

**Rule applied:** prefilter by file mtime ≥ window start, then keep a session only when its record timestamps fall inside both bounds — first record at or before the window end, last record at or after the window start. The mtime prefilter is a superset of the record rule only at the start bound, and only for Claude.

- **Why:** Claude Code session files on this machine were bulk-rewritten without adding timestamped records (the three clusters above). File mtime stopped tracking activity and pulled July and August sessions into the window. Separately, the extractor's window test had no end bound, which let four Codex sessions that began after the window end through.

| rule | how it differs from the 187 included |
|---|---|
| spec: file mtime inside the window | adds back the 14 pre-window Claude sessions, whose mtimes come from the bulk rewrites. It also excludes the 4 post-window Codex sessions, whose mtimes (12:56:44Z–12:57:23Z) fall after the end. |
| record bounds on every file (no mtime prefilter) | adds 01a03e20@dotnet-firewall-analyzer and 01a03e21@dotnet-network-documentation, which the prefilter hid |
| applied: mtime prefilter, then record bounds | the 187; both differences above count as truncation |

- **End bound:** the four Codex sessions the extractor let through began 12:56:44Z–12:57:24Z on 2026-09-09. The end bound excludes them. Codex had no same-second mtime clusters but still lost these four to the end bound.
- **Start bound:** the mtime prefilter loses nothing only for Claude (re-derived).
  - **Claude:** 0 of 439 session files dated before the window start contain a later record, and no in-window file has a record more than 60 s past its mtime.
  - **Codex:** rollout mtime stays near file creation. 466 of 504 in-window rollouts have records more than 60 s past their mtime, by up to 98 h.
  - **Result:** the prefilter hid 2 Codex parents that began minutes before the start and ran into the window: 01a03e20@dotnet-firewall-analyzer (12:52:48Z → 13:40:45Z) and 01a03e21@dotnet-network-documentation (12:53:15Z → 13:12:53Z), both on 2026-08-26.
  - These 2 qualify under the applied rule but were never extracted. They have no child rollouts and hold 15 in-window call occurrences (9 + 6). They are not in the 170 or in any count in this report, and are carried as truncation rows 1–2 rather than folded into the coverage counts, which would require a rerun.
- **Straddlers kept:** claude 5051da13 (2026-08-25T21:48Z → 2026-08-26T16:23Z) crosses the start bound. Sessions that began before the end bound and ran past it are also kept.
- **Extractor note:** each Claude subagent transcript was window-tested by its own mtime, which dropped the pre-window sessions' subagent transcripts. For those sessions that agrees with the record-timestamp rule. In-window sessions keep their subagent evidence.
- **Rerun fix:** apply the record bounds to every rollout, with no mtime prefilter for Codex.

| agent | included before (mtime prefilter) | included after (record bounds) | removed | bound |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| claude | 31 | 17 | 14 | pre-window |
| codex | 174 | 170 | 4 | post-window |
| both | 205 | 187 | 18 | mixed |

Claude's share of included sessions: 15% before, 9% after.

**Method caveats**

1. **Codex has no namespace records for Roslyn.** Codex reaches Roslyn only through the node-REPL exec tool: a `custom_tool_call` named `exec` whose JS input awaits `tools.mcp__roslyn__<tool>({...})`, with the result in a `custom_tool_call_output` keyed by call_id. No `function_call` record carries a roslyn namespace in this window. Included rollouts show only `collaboration` (2,496) and `clock` (2) namespaces, plus 742 un-namespaced function calls (re-derived). The spec's namespace-based extraction rule therefore finds nothing.
2. **Codex call counts are static, everywhere in this report.** They count occurrences of `tools.mcp__roslyn__X(` inside exec scripts, not runtime invocations, and several occurrences can share one call_id — so a Codex "call" count is an upper bound on distinct exec cells. Resolving them to invocations would mean re-deriving from `custom_tool_call_output` call_ids, which was not done this pass. Other dispatch forms are missed:
   - 19 bracket-literal occurrences in 3 included sessions are uncounted (re-derived): 01a05d29@roslyn-backed-mcp 13, 01a03fc6@dotnet-network-documentation 3, 01a05d28@tradewise 3.
   - 01a04322@dotnet-network-documentation called `mcp__roslyn__server_info` through a handle, leaving 0 static occurrences in its file, so the filter dropped it as discoverability-only.
   - 01a08402@snipcue called `server_heartbeat` through `tools[ALL_TOOLS.find(...).name]` and was dropped the same way (truncation row 4).
3. **Codex totals:** the 3,238 static occurrences are 3,290 in the mtime window minus 52 from the four post-window sessions. The Codex per-tool, error-class and catalog-lookup breakdowns still include those 52 occurrences (1.6%) and were not recomputed; each such cell is tagged in the harness facts. The error-class breakdown includes the 31 s cutoff and output truncation counts in the harness facts.
4. **Claude prefixes:** `mcp__plugin_roslyn-mcp_roslyn__` (517 calls) and `mcp__roslyn__` (155 calls), 672 total. Matching is prefix-agnostic, and every tool name is normalized to `mcp__roslyn__<tool>`.

### 0a. Normalize

One record per Roslyn MCP invocation from either source.

| agent | field | Claude source | Codex source |
|---|---|---|---|
| both | agent | `claude` (`~/.claude/projects`) | `codex` (`~/.codex`) |
| both | tool | `tool_use.name` matching `mcp__\S*roslyn\S*__\S+`, prefix stripped → `mcp__roslyn__<tool>` | `<tool>` from `tools.mcp__roslyn__<tool>(` in the `exec` input → `mcp__roslyn__<tool>` |
| both | call_id | `tool_use.id` (`toolu_…`) | exec `call_id` (`call_…`), shared by every Roslyn call in the cell |
| both | inputs | `tool_use.input` object | object literal passed to the tool inside the JS source string |
| both | result | `tool_result` matched by `tool_use_id` | `custom_tool_call_output` matched by `call_id`: cell status, wall time, then the tool output |
| both | errored | `is_error` or a server error envelope in the result | server error envelope in the output, the `Script running` cutoff, `Warning: truncated output`, or a transport or script error |
| both | ts | record `timestamp` | record `timestamp` |
| both | repo | project dir decoded against real `C:/Code-Repo/*` names | ordinal-0 `session_meta.payload.cwd` |
| both | session_id | `.jsonl` basename (UUID) | ordinal-0 `session_meta.payload.id`, never a later replayed record |
| both | is_subagent | transcript under `<session>/subagents/` | ordinal-0 `thread_source` is `subagent` or `guardian_review` |
| both | parent_session_id | the `<session>` directory name | root of the ordinal-0 `parent_thread_id` chain |

**Aggregate mix (187 included sessions):** refactoring 69 (37%), release-operational 14 (7%), planning-docs 12 (6%), mixed 92 (49%).

**Per-harness mix:** claude (17): refactoring 0 (0%), release-operational 4 (24%), planning-docs 1 (6%), mixed 12 (71%). codex (170): refactoring 69 (41%), release-operational 10 (6%), planning-docs 11 (6%), mixed 80 (47%).

**Sample balance note:** one harness is under 20% of included sessions, so the report mostly characterizes the other (see §5).

## 2. Task inventory (aggregated, with agent + session ids)

- **Row key:** one row per (tool family, domain, right-tool verdict), sorted by counted occurrences (desc). Every in-window task item is counted in exactly one row. `Agent(s)` carries the per-agent occurrence split. Domain labels are normalized: qualifiers in parentheses are dropped, and `git` and `gh` are merged into `git / gh`.
- **Counting basis:** an occurrence is one recorded tool use inside a task item as the per-session extraction counted it (one shell command, one `apply_patch` edit, one tool call), summed across sessions. It is not a distinct resolved `call_id`, and it is not one per Codex exec cell — a single cell can carry several. Codex figures inherit §0 method caveat 2: a Roslyn tool use is counted from its static `tools.mcp__roslyn__<tool>(` call site inside an exec script, so several occurrences can share one `call_id` and handle/bracket dispatch is not counted at all. These counts were not re-derived against `custom_tool_call_output` call_ids in this pass. Codex rows therefore carry static call-site occurrences, not resolved invocations, and the unit differs from §2a's per-call counts — the two sections' totals are not comparable.
- **Tool families:** each item's recorded tool goes to the family of the first tool it names, ignoring shell wrappers (`bash`, `exec_command`, `exec`). A Roslyn row takes the category of its first Roslyn tool. Families: `git / gh`, `shell text tools (rg, grep, sed, cat, get-content, Read)`, `apply_patch`, `dotnet CLI, just and eng/*.ps1 scripts`, `Roslyn navigation / search`, `Roslyn workspace / server lifecycle`, `Codex collaboration (spawn_agent, wait_agent)`, `Roslyn validation / diagnostics`, `Edit / Write`, `helper scripts and skills (node, python, script-bridge)`, `Roslyn metrics / analysis`, `Roslyn refactor / edit (preview-apply)`, `Roslyn test tools`, `Codex ALL_TOOLS lookup`, `other shell and harness tools`, `none`. Roslyn rows name up to 6 `mcp__roslyn__<tool>` names recorded for the row, most-used first.
- **Session(s):** distinct session count, then every session behind the row, most occurrences first; mixed-harness rows group ids by harness. Ids are 8-char prefixes, widened to 13 chars (`01a06a0e-dbef`) where two in-window Codex sessions share a prefix. Every id resolves to exactly one transcript in its harness (`~/.claude/projects/*/*.jsonl` or `~/.codex/{sessions,archived_sessions}`), and every session is one of the 187 in-window sessions (claude 17, codex 170).
- **Task:** the 4 most frequent task wordings — plus any wording another section cites by name, tagged `[cited by §2b]` — with per-wording `(xN)` counts, each cut at ~200 chars (`…`); a wording that carries a transcript quote is always shown in full with its `[agent id, path#Lline, record]` locator. `+N more occurrences under M other wordings` closes the gap to the row total.
- **Right tool?:** `yes`; `no`; `missed opportunity` (a Roslyn tool covered the task and was bypassed); `discoverability` (Codex: the harness never surfaced the covering tool; a separate verdict from model judgment).

[claude f41740fa, C:/Users/daryl/.claude/projects/C--Code-Repo-DotNet-Network-Documentation/f41740fa-ffa0-4213-aef0-822c84d2bfa1.jsonl#L275, prose record, no call_id]). - **codex 01a05db7@dotnet-network-documentation (subagent 01a05dca) — IOS route-grammar review (6 occurrences).** The per-item extraction verdict carried into the row is **missed opportunity** ("ParseShowIpRouteIos\|show ip route\|Routing Table:\|Gateway of" [codex 01a05db7, ~/.codex/archived_sessions/rollout-2026-09-01T11-26-19-01a05dca-fda8-7e62-bd84-29a0924ed9de.jsonl#L99, call_dgOr6IaesewnJNONJexj0QRU]). §2b’s own entry reads the same fallback as reasonable for a mixed-language search and attributes the root cause to the upstream `mcp__roslyn__symbol_search` truncation in §2a — a `yes`-shaped reading, not a missed opportunity. The divergence is surfaced, not merged: the occurrence stays counted in the missed-opportunity row, and this pass did not re-adjudicate the verdict.

## 2a. Roslyn MCP issues encountered

Locator prefixes: `CC/` = `~/.claude/projects/`, `CX/` = `~/.codex/archived_sessions/`. Each quote is tagged agent, session (8-char), `path#Lline`, call_id (or "prose"). Notes N1–N7 follow the table.

**Counting basis** (applies to every count in this section, table and distribution read):

| Dimension | Basis |
|---|---|
| Scope | Verified issue keys only (N1–N6). Every session tag in the table is inside the included set (claude 17 / codex 170); none of the 19 window-excluded session ids appears. |
| Unit — claude rows | One count per tool call, keyed by the `toolu_` tool-use id; a repeated id (e.g. the same result echoed in a parent transcript) is counted once. The Claude result-size row's 36 was re-derived on this basis this pass (N3). |
| Unit — codex rows | One count per Roslyn **call-site occurrence** inside an exec cell. The Codex extraction matched call-site text (`tools.mcp__roslyn__<tool>(`), not resolved `custom_tool_call_output` call_ids, so several occurrences can share one call_id: the Codex client-limit totals (174, 78) are call-site occurrences and an upper bound on distinct invocations, not per-call counts. The per-tool breakdowns sum to those totals. |
| Session figures | Harness-tagged `session@repo` tags. One 8-char prefix can name rollouts in two repos (e.g. `01a06a0e@dotnet-network-documentation` and `01a06a0e@tradewise`), so a tag count can exceed the distinct prefixes. |
| Window | The record-timestamp window from §0 (mtime prefilter, then both record bounds). |

| Tool | Agents (`claude` / `codex` / `both`) | Sessions (harness-tagged) | Inputs (summarized) | Symptom (verbatim quote; one per harness when `both`) | Impact | Workaround | Repro confidence | Attribution |

**Distribution read.** The 139 verified issue keys collapse to **64 distinct failure modes: 51 server-side, 8 harness-specific, 5 unknown**. 30 modes are Claude-only, 30 Codex-only, and 4 reproduced in both harnesses. All 4 cross-harness modes are server-side: stale workspace-id NotFound, `mcp__roslyn__compile_check` file-scope widening, `mcp__roslyn__project_diagnostics` severity totals, and `mcp__roslyn__workspace_load` `.sln`/`.slnx` FileNotFound. Neither harness was clean, but the friction differs in kind. By volume, two Codex client limits dominate — in call-site occurrences, not resolved call_ids (see the counting basis). The exec-cell 31 s cutoff hit 174 occurrences across 54 session tags (led by `mcp__roslyn__compile_check` 47, `mcp__roslyn__test_run` 36, `mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload` 32). The output-token cap hit 78 occurrences across 35 tags (led by `mcp__roslyn__symbol_search` 30). Because occurrences can share a call_id, those two totals are upper bounds and are not directly comparable to the Claude per-call figures below. Only stale-workspace NotFound comes close on the server side (131 failures, 127 of them Claude tool calls in one Workflow-tool session). Claude's client-side load is smaller: 36 result-size diversions across 4 sessions plus 3 one-shot modes. Its 21 Claude-only server-side modes cluster on worktree readiness (phantom `mcp__roslyn__compile_check` errors in 5 sessions, the shell-routing restore hint in 4), false positives in the dead-code/duplication analyzers, and `mcp__roslyn__server_info` update-check and recycle metadata. All 5 unknown modes are Claude-only; one of them is the `mcp__roslyn__server_info` version-after-release row, re-attributed because the server reports its launch version accurately. Codex's 26 Codex-only server-side modes cluster on the preview/apply refactoring family: 9 modes, 7 of them rejections whose top-level message names no concrete cause. Validation timeouts are the other cluster (git-status 10 s in 6 sessions, the 25 s internal phase budget in 3). By tool, `mcp__roslyn__workspace_load` appears in 9 modes; `mcp__roslyn__compile_check`, `mcp__roslyn__test_run`, and `mcp__roslyn__project_diagnostics` in 7 each; `mcp__roslyn__symbol_search` and `mcp__roslyn__server_info` in 6. Server-fixable effort concentrates on readiness gating, error-message specificity, and response size, the last of which also drives both harnesses' truncation modes.

**Notes**

- **N1 Merges** (same mode under different keys):

## 2b. Missing tool gaps

Every tool name below was checked against the current Roslyn MCP catalog, including parameter-level schemas. **14 of the 41 rows are discoverability gaps**: an existing tool or route covered the task, or the fix is a server-side discovery change, and each row names the tool. Judgment-only candidates (the tool existed and was reachable, but the agent chose not to use it) are not missing-tool gaps; they are listed under **Excluded from this table** below with an explicit verdict, not carried as table rows. Session flags: **recurring** = 3 or more sessions; **cross-harness** = seen in both Claude and Codex. Short session ids are 8 characters, so two different sessions can share a prefix; the `@repo` tag tells them apart (e.g. `01a06a0e@tradewise` vs `01a06a0e@dotnet-network-documentation`). Every quote was re-checked this pass against the JSONL line and call_id cited next to it. Evidence from sessions outside the included set (§1) is excluded.

## 3. Recurring friction patterns

**Lens.** §1 splits 37% refactoring and 49% mixed, and Codex accounts for 170 of the 187 included sessions. The primary lens is therefore refactoring: symbol precision, preview/apply, and validation/test-fixup. The release lens covers Claude's release-operational sessions (3#install-layer-version-drift). Planning-docs work (12 sessions, 6%) is out of scope. Its only recurring signal is reference-doc version drift, seen in 2 Codex sessions and folded into 3#install-layer-version-drift.

**Counting bases.** §3 uses three kinds of count. They differ in scope and unit from the totals in §0 and 2a/§5, so do not compare or add them across sections. Per-call totals for the 187 included sessions come from 2a.

| agent | tag | scope | unit | window | use |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| both | `rows` | 2a rows given to §3 | sessions per 2a row | corrected window (§0) | Authoritative spread and ranking |
| both | `cluster` | friction-key clusters | keys / sessions | corrected window | Width only; never quoted |
| claude | `scan` | top-level + subagent JSONL with ≥1 Roslyn call | results or calls, and sessions | file mtime 2026-08-26T12:55Z → 2026-09-09T12:55Z (28 sessions) | **Upper bound.** The corrected window keeps 17 Claude sessions, so up to 11 scanned sessions fall outside it (bulk-mtime rewrite, §0). No quoted Claude session is on §0's excluded list. |
| codex | `scan` | 170 sessions / 244 rollout files enumerated, child threads rolled up by `parent_thread_id` | exec cells or results, and sessions | file mtime, same bounds | **Lower bound.** §0 rolls the same 170 families up to 347 rollouts (170 parents + 177 children), so this scan reaches ~103 fewer child rollouts. Every Codex `scan` session spread and tally below is a floor, marked `≥`. Tool tallies are static `tools.mcp__roslyn__*(` call sites, so one cell can count several tools. `ALL_TOOLS` lookups also count sessions with no Roslyn call. |

**Reconciliation with §0 / 2a.** Where a §3 scan total disagreed with §0 or 2a for the same phenomenon, §3 keeps only session spreads and 2a row counts. It drops its own total and tally rather than choose between bases. The one exception is the Claude result-size cap, recounted from source for this revision.

| phenomenon | §3 scan basis | §0 / 2a basis | kept in §3 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Codex 31s exec cutoff | cutoff cells over the mtime-window rollouts | §0 counts Roslyn-bearing cells; 2a counts calls per row | Session spread (≥76 sessions) and 2a row session counts. Cell total and per-tool tally dropped. |
| Codex output truncation | truncated cells over the mtime-window rollouts | §0 counts cells; 2a counts calls per row | Session spread (≥100 sessions) and 2a rows. Cell total and tally dropped. |
| Claude result-size cap | **recounted**: every Roslyn `tool_result` in b0eb94cb, ca03a950, 9417cc0a and 9c0caec1 (main + subagent transcripts) whose text carries `exceeds maximum allowed tokens` or `Output too large (`, deduped by `tool_use_id` | 2a counts calls per row in the same sessions | **36 calls.** One basis, per tool call, both failure shapes: 24 rejected as a tool error + 12 diverted to a persisted-output file. A narrower basis that counts only the error-rejected shape gives 24; that is the same phenomenon at a different unit, not a second population. |
| Codex rollout denominator | 244 rollout files enumerated for the 170 included Codex families | §0 rolls the same 170 families up to 347 rollouts (170 parents + 177 children) | Session spreads only, marked `≥`. The scan covers a narrower child set (~103 child rollouts short), so every Codex `scan` spread and tally in this section is a lower bound. Not reconciled: §3 cannot tell whether the gap is unenumerated children or a different parent/child join. |
| Generic NotFound in b0eb94cb | envelopes across that session's subagent files | 2a counts calls per row | File distribution (Workflow vs direct subagents) only. Envelope total and per-tool tally dropped; use 2a. |

**References and quotes.**
- **2a references.** A 2a reference names the tool and, where §3 has it, the 2a failure-mode slug: `2a#mcp__roslyn__<tool>` (`<slug>`). A reference without a slug covers every 2a row for that tool within the named symptom.
- **Tool names.** Tool names are normalized to `mcp__roslyn__<tool>`; the b0eb94cb and 9c0caec1 transcripts register the same server under the plugin prefix `mcp__plugin_roslyn-mcp_roslyn__`, counted as the same tool. Tool cells that are not Roslyn calls (`prose`, `ToolSearch`, `Bash`, `exec (…)`, `wait → …`) are client-side records. A `wait → …` cell is a poll whose target cell ran the named Roslyn tools.
- **Quote bytes.** Quotes are raw JSONL bytes with escapes preserved, so `rg -F` against the locator file finds them. Each quote is a substring of the cited line. Its call_id or uuid, where one exists, is on that line.
- **Session tags.** Session tags are `agent:8char@repo`. Codex 8-char prefixes collide (e.g. `01a055af` = 4 sessions), so the UUID in the locator is authoritative. Codex child threads are tagged `(child <id>)`.

Claude `scan` figures carry `≤` because they are upper bounds, and Codex `scan` figures carry `≥` because they are lower bounds (see Counting bases). The one exact `scan` figure is the recounted Claude result-size cap (36 calls), which is a full enumeration of its 4 sessions.

**Cross-harness parity (reserved slot).** 3#oversized-result-truncation-parity. Two other patterns carry secondary parity evidence:
- **3#codex-exec-31s-cutoff-forces-poll.** Codex returns a placeholder at 31s; Claude moves the call to the background at 120s.
- **3#workspace-handle-lost-across-agent-boundary.** Claude in-process subagents share the server; Claude Workflow subagents and Codex child threads did not.

### 3#codex-exec-31s-cutoff-forces-poll — Codex exec cells cut Roslyn calls at ~31s

- **What happened.** If a Codex exec cell is still waiting on a Roslyn call at ~31s, it returns a `Script running` placeholder. The model then spends a `wait(cell_id)` turn on a result the server is still computing or has already produced. The 2a cutoff rows concentrate on gate-holding validation tools (see Spread → rows). Server timeouts can stack underneath the cutoff, e.g. the internal 25s phase timeout in `mcp__roslyn__validate_recent_git_changes`.

| agent | session | tool | locator | call_id | quote |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| codex | codex:01a06a0f@roslyn-backed-mcp | mcp__roslyn__workspace_load | `C:/Users/daryl/.codex/archived_sessions/rollout-2026-09-03T20-36-07-01a06a0f-119e-7e40-b8ea-7e5ab25c9ac3.jsonl#L7267` | call_55Vs2sWuj4ohBrprDvYiTziI | `Script running with cell ID 720\nWall time 31.1 seconds` |
| codex | codex:01a04941@tradewise | mcp__roslyn__project_diagnostics + mcp__roslyn__find_dead_fields + mcp__roslyn__find_dead_locals + mcp__roslyn__get_complexity_metrics | `C:/Users/daryl/.codex/archived_sessions/rollout-2026-08-28T11-44-04-01a04941-cda6-7d33-91b6-086769444f35.jsonl#L267` | call_seECiMDl7Py4offQTNbhioq5 | `Script running with cell ID 36\nWall time 31.0 seconds` |

| codex | codex:01a06a0e@dotnet-network-documentation (child 01a06a5f) | mcp__roslyn__compile_check | `C:/Users/daryl/.codex/archived_sessions/rollout-2026-09-03T22-03-55-01a06a5f-740c-7463-a7db-d19534a250ab.jsonl#L443` | call_ws1gipa1m4uVFCoe5rihLoel | `Script running with cell ID 58\nWall time 31.1 seconds` |
| codex | codex:01a048f7@tradewise | mcp__roslyn__validate_recent_git_changes + mcp__roslyn__project_diagnostics | `C:/Users/daryl/.codex/archived_sessions/rollout-2026-08-28T10-23-09-01a048f7-b7a5-77c1-af06-4f5c8a249500.jsonl#L363` | call_8jZMo0wsbxRCtdbcGCueP20A | `Script running with cell ID 49\nWall time 31.0 seconds` |
| codex | codex:01a05438@tradewise | wait → mcp__roslyn__validate_recent_git_changes | `C:/Users/daryl/.codex/archived_sessions/rollout-2026-08-30T14-49-58-01a05438-b9ab-7591-b840-29444c3cc9ff.jsonl#L282` | call_8YWQhTcxk9ekZIhO0sxqdSTq | `exceeded the internal timeout of 25 second(s); retryable=true` |
| claude | claude:a3c84938@roslyn-backed-mcp | mcp__roslyn__test_run | `C:/Users/daryl/.claude/projects/C--Code-Repo-Roslyn-Backed-MCP/a3c84938-f8b8-4165-9a01-dd8e8e1790b5.jsonl#L748` | toolu_01Szhbpt1VjWXzkaKPk92YjD | `MCP tool \"roslyn/test_run\" is still running after 120s. It was moved to the background as task k21d3sus7` |

- **Spread.**
  - rows (cutoff symptom): 2a#mcp__roslyn__compile_check (18 sessions), 2a#mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload (10), 2a#mcp__roslyn__test_run (9, plus 2 one-shot variants), 2a#mcp__roslyn__validate_recent_git_changes (8), 2a#mcp__roslyn__project_diagnostics (7), 2a#mcp__roslyn__workspace_load (6). One-shot rows exist on `mcp__roslyn__symbol_search`, `mcp__roslyn__symbol_impact_sweep`, `mcp__roslyn__apply_text_edit`, `mcp__roslyn__server_heartbeat`, `mcp__roslyn__preview_multi_file_edit` and `mcp__roslyn__workspace_status`.
  - scan: cutoff cells occur in ≥76 of 170 Codex Roslyn sessions and in 0 Claude sessions. The cell total is dropped (see Reconciliation).
  - cluster: 45 keys / 49 sessions, all Codex.
  - Phases: 01a06a0f@roslyn-backed-mcp mixed; 01a04941@tradewise refactoring; 01a06e27@roslyn-backed-mcp refactoring; 01a06a0e@dotnet-network-documentation mixed; 01a048f7 and 01a05438@tradewise mixed.
- **Harness sensitivity.**
  - Codex harness only: every 2a cutoff row is attributed `harness-specific`.
  - Claude calls the same tools as blocking `tool_use` with no per-cell wall clock. Its ceiling is 120s, and past it Claude moves the call to the background rather than returning a placeholder (a3c84938, 1 call in the window).
  - So the 31s boundary is client plumbing; server latency only decides which cells cross it.
- **Why it recurs.** Codex batches several Roslyn calls into one cell, sometimes with `exec_command` test runs. The calls that hold the workspace gate are the validation steps every edit loop runs. The scan finds 178 cells chaining `mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload` + `mcp__roslyn__compile_check` across ≥69 sessions. That self-inflicted part is 3#defensive-reload-after-out-of-band-edit.
- **Fix (codex-harness).** When a cell's only outstanding awaits are MCP tool calls, keep it yielding until those calls settle instead of returning `Script running` at 31s. The wait stays bounded by each MCP call's own timeout.

### 3#defensive-reload-after-out-of-band-edit — agents reload by hand before every check although the server auto-reloads stale workspaces

- **What happened.** After `apply_patch` edits (not Roslyn `*_apply` routes), agents call `mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload` right before `mcp__roslyn__compile_check` or `mcp__roslyn__project_diagnostics`. They pay the load gate every round, whether or not the workspace is stale. The default stale policy (`StalenessPolicy.AutoReload`, ExecutionGateOptions.cs:59) already reloads a watcher-flagged workspace inside the next call and stamps `_meta.staleAction`. Each reload also invalidates outstanding preview tokens.

| agent | session | tool | locator | call_id | quote |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| codex | codex:01a053cf@dotnet-firewall-analyzer | mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload + mcp__roslyn__compile_check | `C:/Users/daryl/.codex/archived_sessions/rollout-2026-08-30T12-54-35-01a053cf-1559-7790-a369-5e252a8faf5c.jsonl#L393` | call_afB3XiceJgX0xymDy0S4ZprE | `const reload=await tools.mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload({workspaceId:ws,autoRestore:false,verbose:false});\nconst compile=await tools.mcp__roslyn__compile_check({workspaceId:ws,` |
| codex | codex:01a048f7@tradewise | mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload + mcp__roslyn__compile_check | `C:/Users/daryl/.codex/archived_sessions/rollout-2026-08-28T10-23-09-01a048f7-b7a5-77c1-af06-4f5c8a249500.jsonl#L140` | call_oFPhOIWHzTiDt8KakP3BLc2W | `\\\"gateMode\\\": \\\"load\\\",\\r\\n    \\\"queuedMs\\\": 5580,\\r\\n    \\\"heldMs\\\": 10345` |
| codex | codex:01a05d28@tradewise | mcp__roslyn__workspace_status + mcp__roslyn__compile_check | `C:/Users/daryl/.codex/archived_sessions/rollout-2026-09-01T08-29-13-01a05d28-da2b-7d12-a5c9-2915d2ad8b03.jsonl#L909` | call_hrWYv1xSDxGfskNuRxJah6rN | `\\\"staleAction\\\": \\\"auto-reloaded\\\",\\r\\n    \\\"staleReloadMs\\\": 8104` |
| codex | codex:01a0601f@tradewise | mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload + mcp__roslyn__validate_workspace | `C:/Users/daryl/.codex/archived_sessions/rollout-2026-09-01T22-17-50-01a0601f-7a01-70b2-b11b-6c2bb0a3fea6.jsonl#L1198` | call_odKrVo4ZH04GWbMX6KSstJ0E | `\\\"staleAction\\\": \\\"auto-reloaded\\\",\\r\\n    \\\"staleReloadMs\\\": 5920` |
| codex | codex:01a03fee@dotnet-firewall-analyzer | mcp__roslyn__apply_composite_preview | `C:/Users/daryl/.codex/archived_sessions/rollout-2026-08-26T16-16-51-01a03fee-d1ba-7530-95be-a6e398ef3204.jsonl#L231` | call_7dcBJ4rzeb5T6Am9GTEBUdXB | `The preview token has expired because the workspace was reloaded after the preview was created.` |
| codex | codex:01a06a0f@roslyn-backed-mcp (child 01a06a68) | mcp__roslyn__test_run | `C:/Users/daryl/.codex/archived_sessions/rollout-2026-09-03T22-13-18-01a06a68-09e8-7db1-8908-aeb70355431a.jsonl#L226` | call_O1cucvOnmXcHFvRPjzvQtUb8 | `Call workspace_reload if the state is stale, then retry.` |

- **Spread.**
  - rows: 2a#mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload (`gate-hold-latency`; `codex-exec-31s-cutoff` ×10), 2a#mcp__roslyn__apply_composite_preview (`PreviewTokenStale`), 2a#mcp__roslyn__test_run (`invalidoperation`).
  - scan, codex (lower bound): 187 `staleAction: auto-reloaded` stamps in ≥46 sessions show the server reloading on its own after out-of-band edits. Alongside them are 447 explicit `mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload` call sites in ≥117 sessions, and 178 reload + compile_check cells in ≥69 sessions.
  - scan, claude (upper bound): 14 auto-reload stamps in ≤4 sessions, alongside 9 explicit reload calls in ≤4 sessions.
  - cluster: workspace-stale-auto-reload-latency 16 keys / 18 sessions (claude + codex); reload-before-every-call 7 keys / 9 sessions (codex).
  - Phases: 01a053cf, 01a0601f and 01a03fee refactoring; 01a048f7, 01a05d28@tradewise and 01a06a0f mixed.
- **Harness sensitivity.**
  - Both harnesses reload defensively; Codex has roughly 50× the reload call sites.
  - The cost is server-side (the gate's own `queuedMs`/`heldMs` in `_meta`), and the trigger is server guidance, not client plumbing.
  - Codex feels it more for two reasons: apply_patch is its default edit path, and the reload and check share one 31s cell (3#codex-exec-31s-cutoff-forces-poll).
- **Why it recurs.**
  - Only the refactoring-workflow prompt mentions auto-reload (RoslynPrompts.RefactoringWorkflows.cs:324); none of the default surfaces an agent reads do.
  - ServerInstructions.cs:10-19 lists `mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload` under lifecycle only.
  - Its description (WorkspaceTools.cs:103) opens "Reload a loaded workspace from disk to pick up file changes."
  - The InvalidOperation envelope says "Call workspace_reload if the state is stale" (ToolErrorHandler.cs:175).
  - Each apply_patch round re-triggers the habit.
- **Fix (server guidance).** State the AutoReload default where agents decide to reload:
  - In the `mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload` `[Description]` (WorkspaceTools.cs:103).
  - In one ServerInstructions sentence; the text is ~1,150 of the 2,048-char `ClientCharacterLimit`. The sentence: workspace-scoped calls auto-reload a watcher-flagged stale workspace and report it in `_meta.staleAction`. Reload explicitly only when a response reports `staleAction: warn` or `restoreRequired: true`, and in the latter case pass `autoRestore=true`.

### 3#oversized-result-truncation-parity — same oversized payload, loud failure in Claude, silent truncation in Codex (reserved cross-harness parity slot)

- **What happened.** The same high-fan-out tools overflow both clients' result limits, and each client fails differently.
  - Claude Code rejects the result as a tool error and saves the payload to a `tool-results` file (quoted rows: 50,584–147,962 chars), or returns a `<persisted-output>` stub naming the same file (quoted row: 61KB). Across the 4 affected sessions, 24 of 36 oversized calls took the error shape and 12 took the stub shape.
  - Codex keeps the head inline behind `Warning: truncated output (original token count: N)` (quoted rows: 10,095–13,319 tokens) and drops the tail with no error.
  - Item-count limits do not bound bytes. Claude b0eb94cb called `mcp__roslyn__find_references` with `summary:true, limit:200` and got 58,997 chars. Codex 01a06417 called it with `limit:100, summary:false` and got 12,808 tokens.

- **Spread.**
  - rows:
    - 2a#mcp__roslyn__find_references (claude ×1; codex ×7 + 1, including `batched-with-large-read`)
    - 2a#mcp__roslyn__test_related_files (claude ×1; codex ×2 + 1)
    - 2a#mcp__roslyn__symbol_search (codex ×19), 2a#mcp__roslyn__get_source_text (×3 + 1), 2a#mcp__roslyn__compile_check (×2), 2a#mcp__roslyn__get_complexity_metrics (×2 + 1), 2a#mcp__roslyn__semantic_search (×2), 2a#mcp__roslyn__find_references_bulk (×2)
    - 2a#mcp__roslyn__get_coupling_metrics, 2a#mcp__roslyn__get_cohesion_metrics, 2a#mcp__roslyn__find_unused_symbols, 2a#mcp__roslyn__find_dead_fields
    - one-shot truncation rows on `mcp__roslyn__workspace_load`, `mcp__roslyn__rename_preview`, `mcp__roslyn__impact_analysis`, `mcp__roslyn__document_symbols`, `mcp__roslyn__get_symbol_outline` and `mcp__roslyn__project_diagnostics`. Per-call counts are in these 2a rows.
  - scan, claude (exact, full enumeration of the 4 sessions): **36 oversized Roslyn calls** — 24 rejected with `exceeds maximum allowed tokens`, 12 returned as a `<persisted-output>` stub. By session: b0eb94cb 32, ca03a950 2, 9417cc0a 1, 9c0caec1 1. b0eb94cb's 32 are `mcp__roslyn__get_coupling_metrics` 19 and `mcp__roslyn__find_references` 13, one per Workflow-subagent transcript, and none in its main transcript. This is the single per-call basis for this phenomenon; see Reconciliation for how it relates to the narrower error-only count of 24.
  - scan, codex (lower bound): truncated Roslyn cells in ≥100 sessions. The cell total and per-tool tally are dropped (see Reconciliation).
  - cluster: symbol_search-output-truncated-by-harness 5 sessions (codex); claude-truncation-forces-refetch 3 sessions (claude).
  - Phases: claude b0eb94cb planning-docs; 9417cc0a, ca03a950 and 9c0caec1 mixed. codex 01a06417 mixed; 01a04da1 and 01a055af@roslyn-backed-mcp refactoring.
- **Harness sensitivity.** Cross-harness parity: the server writes the same payload for both clients, and the client decides how it fails.
  - Claude fails loudly in both of its shapes: 24 of 36 as an explicit tool error, 12 as a stub that names the file. Either way the full payload is on disk for the model to re-read or narrow.
  - Codex fails silently: partial JSON inline, so a reference list missing its tail reads as complete. Codex's cap also applies per exec cell, so batched calls share one budget (2a#mcp__roslyn__find_references, `01a055af` `batched-with-large-read`).
  - 2a attributes the symptoms `harness-specific`, but the shared root cause is server response size, and only a server fix removes it for both clients.
- **Why it recurs.** Every affected tool bounds rows, not bytes:

  | tool | row cap | source |
  |---|---|---|
  | `mcp__roslyn__find_references` | `limit=100` | SymbolTools.cs:270 |
  | `mcp__roslyn__test_related_files` | `maxResults=100` | ValidationTools.cs:468 |
  | `mcp__roslyn__get_coupling_metrics` | `limit=100` | CouplingAnalysisTools.cs:24 |
  | `mcp__roslyn__find_unused_symbols` | `limit=50` | AdvancedAnalysisTools.cs:24 |
  | `mcp__roslyn__find_dead_fields` | `limit=50` | AdvancedAnalysisTools.cs:388 |

  Per-row preview text, absolute paths and handles push even default calls past both limits on solutions with 10+ projects. The b0eb94cb tally is the clearest case: the same two tools, called once per fan-out subagent, overflowed on all 32 calls.
- **Fix (server).** Add a serialized-size budget to these list tools. When the JSON would exceed a character ceiling set below the smallest observed client limit, stop adding rows and return `hasMore` plus the next `offset`. That is the paging contract `mcp__roslyn__find_references` already exposes (SymbolTools.cs:271). Both clients then get complete, paged JSON instead of a saved-to-file error or a silently cut tail.

### 3#opaque-preview-refusal-forces-apply-patch — refactoring previews refuse without a reason, so agents hand-edit

- **What happened.** Refactoring previews refuse with an InvalidOperation envelope that gives no reason. The agent cannot correct its inputs, so it falls back to hand edits with apply_patch. Those hand edits in turn feed 3#defensive-reload-after-out-of-band-edit.

Check the tool contract and retry.` |

- **Spread.**
  - rows: 2a#mcp__roslyn__move_type_to_file_preview (intermittent, 2); 2a#mcp__roslyn__preview_multi_file_edit (`invalidoperation` intermittent 3; `invalidargument` deterministic 4); 2a#mcp__roslyn__extract_type_preview; 2a#mcp__roslyn__extract_method_preview; 2a#mcp__roslyn__change_type_namespace_preview; 2a#mcp__roslyn__symbol_refactor_preview.
  - scan, codex (lower bound): 12 generic InvalidOperation envelopes in ≥7 sessions:

    | tool | envelopes |
    |---|---|
    | `mcp__roslyn__move_type_to_file_preview` | 6 |
    | `mcp__roslyn__preview_multi_file_edit` | 2 |
    | `mcp__roslyn__symbol_refactor_preview` | 1 |

- **Harness sensitivity.** Server-side; only Codex exercised it. The server's ToolErrorHandler produces the envelope text, independent of the client.
- **Why it recurs.** The refactoring services refuse with plain `InvalidOperationException`: 138 throw sites across 11 services in `src/RoslynMcp.Roslyn/Services`. One is `TypeMoveService`, which backs `mcp__roslyn__move_type_to_file_preview`. None of them uses `PublicInvalidOperationException`. ToolErrorHandler.cs:173-176 replaces any plain message that lacks a stale/workspace keyword with the generic text, so the real refusal reason never leaves the server.
- **Fix (server).** In the refactoring services, throw preview refusals as `PublicInvalidOperationException` with a reviewed reason string; that type already travels the public-message path at ToolErrorHandler.cs:159-161. Start with `TypeMoveService`, since `mcp__roslyn__move_type_to_file_preview` produced 6 of the 12 generic envelopes.

### 3#unrestored-worktree-phantom-diagnostics — compile_check runs on a workspace its own load flagged restoreRequired

- **What happened.** Worktree executors load a fresh checkout, and `mcp__roslyn__workspace_load` reports `restoreRequired: true`. `mcp__roslyn__compile_check` runs anyway and returns unresolved-reference errors (CS0246/CS0234 text) that disappear after restore: 46 in one session, 24,654 in another. Validation reads red, and agents chase bugs that don't exist.

| claude | claude:ca7969ce@dotnet-network-documentation | mcp__roslyn__compile_check | `C:/Users/daryl/.claude/projects/C--Code-Repo-DotNet-Network-Documentation/ca7969ce-fe72-49f3-8e39-c1e14fb9495f/subagents/agent-a8d0228deabcb3dd7.jsonl#L124` | toolu_018DLRosx6b51XtfPtjUKXxr | `\"errorCount\": 46,` |

- **Spread.**

  - scan, claude (upper bound): `restoreRequired: true` in 16 results across ≤7 sessions, 15 of them from `mcp__roslyn__workspace_load`. Five Claude sessions have compile_check results with the missing-reference text: 1bec7d73, ca7969ce, 5051da13, 9417cc0a, a78cb38f. All five are among those 7, and none is on §0's excluded list.
  - scan, codex (lower bound): `restoreRequired: true` in 155 results across ≥32 sessions (`mcp__roslyn__workspace_load` 62, `mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload` 59).
  - Phases: every Claude session is a mixed worktree fan-out, with subagent counts 9417cc0a 16, ca7969ce 11, 1bec7d73 12, 5051da13 10 and a78cb38f 20. Codex 01a06c9a@bioremote is refactoring, with 12 subagents.
- **Harness sensitivity.** Server-side; no client plumbing is involved. In this window only Claude has phantom-error rows, but Codex reaches the same unrestored state in ≥32 sessions, against Claude's ≤7.
- **Why it recurs.** WorkspaceManager.cs:1194-1224 computes `session.RestoreRequired` at load, but CompileCheckService.cs and CompileCheckTools.cs never read it. Their only reference to restore is prose in CompileCheckTools.cs:22. Every worktree-isolated executor starts unrestored, so each fan-out run reproduces the problem.
- **Fix (server).** Gate `mcp__roslyn__compile_check` on `session.RestoreRequired`. When it is true, return a structured restore-required result with no diagnostic list. The result names `mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload` with `autoRestore=true` (WorkspaceTools.cs:103) as the recovery.

### 3#workspace-handle-lost-across-agent-boundary — workspace ids handed to child agents go dead, and every miss reads as the same NotFound

- **What happened.** Orchestrators pass a workspaceId to spawned agents as prompt text. In Claude Workflow-tool subagents and Codex child threads, that id is often dead, or the child reloads the workspace from cold. Every miss returns the same generic NotFound, whether the cause is a dead workspace or a stale symbolHandle. So agents retry blind, load again, or fall back to grep. Where the ids in the evidence rows came from:
  - Evidence row 1 passed `942a26bc…`, which first appears in the main session at `C:/Users/daryl/.claude/projects/C--Code-Repo-DotNet-Firewall-Analyzer/b0eb94cb-3d67-42ea-b722-17448f22a81d.jsonl#L89`.

  - **Claude in-process subagents share the parent's server.** 9c0caec1 reused one id 22× across 2 direct-subagent files (0 Workflow-subagent files), and none of its subagent files contains a NotFound. The id is `29b0dfe6…`, first seen at `C:/Users/daryl/.claude/projects/C--Code-Repo-TradeWise/9c0caec1-f143-4947-a731-d32a0d35b65e.jsonl#L194`.
  - **Claude Workflow subagents did not share it.** In b0eb94cb, every file with a NotFound is a Workflow subagent.
  - **Codex child threads load from cold.** 01a05dd8 gets a fresh id at `workspaceVersion: 1`.
  - The client decides whether a child shares server state; the server decides how a miss is reported.
- **Why it recurs.** Fan-out workflows pass workspace ids through prompts: /backlog-remediate executors, and Codex spawn trees of 3–59 children. The server then discards the message that would tell a dead workspace from a stale handle.
  - WorkspaceManager.cs:1367-1371 throws a `KeyNotFoundException` naming the id and the active-session count.
  - ToolErrorHandler.BuildSafeNotFoundMessage (ToolErrorHandler.cs:559-572) turns it into the same generic string a stale symbolHandle gets.
  - Only tracked evictions and host recycles get the separate WorkspaceEvicted envelope (WorkspaceManager.cs:1342-1365, ToolErrorHandler.cs:105-110).
- **Fix (server).** At WorkspaceManager.cs:1367, throw a dedicated public exception for an unknown workspaceId. Give it its own category, separate from symbol/document NotFound, and carry the id, the active-session count and the recovery (`mcp__roslyn__workspace_list` / `mcp__roslyn__workspace_load`). `NotFound` then means only a symbol, document or handle miss.

### 3#per-agent-tool-discovery-tax — every agent rediscovers the Roslyn tools, and the server's router points at a non-tool

- **What happened.** Each agent rediscovers the Roslyn tools before its first call.
  - Claude subagents run ToolSearch `select:` over deferred schemas.
  - Codex sessions filter the static `ALL_TOOLS` catalog in exec cells. Full dumps get truncated (90,949 tokens), and guessed names that don't exist (`mcp__roslyn__read_source`) silently match nothing.
  - The server's own router, `mcp__roslyn__recommend_workflow`, falls back to `discover_capabilities`, which neither client can call as a tool.

- **Spread.**

  - scan, claude (upper bound): 221 Roslyn ToolSearch calls in ≤23 sessions, 198 of them from subagent files. The session count exceeds the 17 included Claude sessions because the scan is mtime-based (see Counting bases).
  - scan, codex (lower bound): `ALL_TOOLS` lookups naming roslyn in 1,392 exec cells across ≥196 sessions. That count includes lookup-only sessions with no Roslyn call, so it exceeds the 170-session call set in one direction while the narrower rollout enumeration (see Reconciliation) holds it down in the other.
  - scan: the `mcp__roslyn__recommend_workflow` generic fallback appears in ≥12 Codex sessions and 0 Claude sessions.
  - cluster: codex-repeated-all-tools-catalog-lookup 14 sessions (codex); lazy-schema-load-per-subagent 13 (both); tool-catalog-description-boilerplate-bloats-broad-lookup 11 (codex); planner-reviewer-subagents-skip-roslyn 3 (claude).
  - Phases: mixed 9417cc0a, ca7969ce and 01a06417; refactoring 01a06c9a and 01a05ef7.
- **Harness sensitivity.** Both harnesses, through different mechanisms.
  - Claude makes narrow exact-name round trips, repeated per subagent; roles that never search never touch Roslyn.
  - Codex filters the whole catalog, bounded by the exec output cap, and a wrong name fails silently.
  - The server's contribution is the same for both: per-tool description size (tracked by the `method-diet-*` backlog rows) and a router fallback that names something that isn't a tool.
- **Why it recurs.** Spawned agents start with no schemas loaded, and the documented discovery path dead-ends in both clients:
  - ServerInstructions.cs:13-17 names lifecycle and validation tools exactly but describes navigation only by category ("symbols, definitions, references").
  - The no-match branch of `mcp__roslyn__recommend_workflow` (WorkflowRecommendationTools.cs:105-110) returns `PrimaryTools: ["discover_capabilities"]`.
  - That name is registered as an MCP prompt (RoslynPrompts.AnalysisWorkflows.cs:90), not a tool.
- **Fix (server).** Replace `discover_capabilities` in the no-match fallback with concrete, callable tools per category. Emit them as the server's bare names; clients see them as e.g. `mcp__roslyn__symbol_search`, `mcp__roslyn__find_references`, `mcp__roslyn__go_to_definition`, `mcp__roslyn__get_source_text`, `mcp__roslyn__compile_check`. One exact-name lookup (ToolSearch `select:` or an `ALL_TOOLS` name filter) then resolves the router's pick.

### 3#install-layer-version-drift — release audits can't trust version identity (release lens)

- **What happened.** In release and install audits, version identity is unreliable in five ways:
  - `mcp__roslyn__server_info` reports `update.updateAvailable:false` while the NuGet check is still `pending`.
  - The running stdio server keeps its launch version until the host restarts.
  - One session can carry two registrations at different versions.
  - Uninstalling the global tool mid-session was denied on the tool-store path; 2a attributes the cause as unknown.
  - Repo reference docs drift from the live server. DotNet-Network-Documentation's `ai_docs/references/tooling/roslyn_mcp_usage.md` cites server version `1.33.2.0`, while the live `mcp__roslyn__server_info` in the same session reports `4.0.0`.

| claude | claude:532d14a3@roslyn-backed-mcp | prose | `C:/Users/daryl/.claude/projects/C--Code-Repo-Roslyn-Backed-MCP/532d14a3-6cd9-4ed9-9edc-3e96b779fce7.jsonl#L806` | prose (uuid 015638d7-b3f1-4456-b637-2b129890a83c) | `` The running server still reports `4.1.1` (pid 47736) because it launched before the cut. `` |

- **Spread.**
  - rows, update check: `pending` + `updateAvailable:false` in 5 Claude sessions. Four are release-operational: 3c572d4b, 532d14a3, cb593a23 and b4a3a083, which is all 4 of Claude's release-operational sessions per §1. The fifth, ee6273ee, is mixed with a release split.
  - rows, doc drift: 2 Codex planning-docs sessions. 01a0451f comments on the drift in prose. 01a03fc6 read the reference doc (L155) after the live `mcp__roslyn__server_info` call (L86) and did not comment on the mismatch. No 2a row backs doc drift.
  - scan, `checkStatus: pending`: claude 17× in ≤12 sessions (upper bound); codex 114× in ≥83 sessions (lower bound).
  - scan, `checkStatus: succeeded`: claude 2× in 1 session; codex 8× in ≥4 sessions. So `pending` occurs in both harnesses.
  - 2a refs: all on 2a#mcp__roslyn__server_info: `update-check-pending-on-first-probe`, `update-check-never-completes`, `update-check-pending-false-negative`, `update-check-pending`, `update-block-reports-no-update-while-check-pending`, `stale-version-until-host-restart`, `duplicate-registration-version-skew`, `previous-pid-overwritten-by-probe-process`, `toolsearch-no-matching-deferred-tools-after-disconnect`, `server-process-locks-tool-store`.
  - Phases: release-operational (Claude update check); planning-docs (Codex doc drift).
- **Harness sensitivity.**
  - The update-block false negative is server-side: every client gets the same JSON.
  - Duplicate registrations come from Claude's registration model: two prefixes in one session (cb593a23).
  - The pinned launch version comes from the host lifecycle and affects any client that keeps a long-lived stdio child.
  - Doc drift is repo documentation, not client plumbing.
- **Why it recurs.** Release audits probe once per fresh process, so they hit this every time:
  - NuGetVersionChecker.GetLatestVersion (NuGetVersionChecker.cs:177-200) starts the NuGet fetch only on first access, so the first `mcp__roslyn__server_info` call of every process reads `pending`.
  - ServerTools.BuildUpdateInfo (ServerTools.cs:281-284) sets `UpdateAvailable` to false whenever `latest` is null, so "unknown" and "up to date" serialize the same way.
- **Fix (server).** Make `update.updateAvailable` nullable and emit null unless `checkStatus` is `succeeded` (ServerTools.cs:281-299).

## 4. Suggested findings (up to 8)

These findings are ranked and informational only; nothing here writes the backlog. Findings are ordered by priority hint, then by whether the failure reproduced in both harnesses, then by cost per incident, then by how many sessions they span.

- **Locators.** `CC/` = `~/.claude/projects/` and `CX/` = `~/.codex/archived_sessions/`. Each `[Qn]` resolves in its finding's locator table, which gives the agent, the harness-tagged session, `path#Lline`, and the call_id (or `prose`).
- **Quote form.** Every quote is verbatim.
  - Quotes containing `\n` or `\\\"` are raw JSONL bytes.
  - JSON-field quotes such as `"errorCount": 24654,` are shown decoded; the raw line has escaped quotes.
  - Every quote and call_id below was re-matched against its line in this pass. Parents of Codex child threads were re-read from `session_meta`.
- **Tool names.** Tools are written as `mcp__roslyn__<tool>`. Verbatim quotes, 2a/2b/§3 anchor ids, and code identifiers keep their original form. `discover_capabilities` is a prompt, not a tool, so it has no prefix.
- **Code anchors.** All were re-read at repo HEAD `bdd54e47`. Code facts stated in a finding are as of that commit.

| rank | agents | id | priority | surface | title |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | both | `unknown-workspace-id-error-category` | high | server | Give an unknown workspaceId its own error, not the generic NotFound |
| 2 | both | `list-tool-response-byte-budget` | high | codex-harness, claude-harness | Keep oversized results retrievable in both clients; add a server byte budget |
| 3 | claude (codex adjacent, see 4.3) | `compile-check-restore-required-gate` | high (single-harness) | server | Gate mcp__roslyn__compile_check on the load's restore/build-required flags |
| 4 | codex | `codex-exec-cell-mcp-await-cutoff` | high (single-harness) | codex-harness | Keep Codex exec cells yielding while only MCP calls are outstanding |
| 5 | both | `preview-refusal-public-reasons` | medium | server | Replace generic InvalidOperation/InvalidArgument text with reviewed reasons |
| 6 | both | `diagnostics-scope-and-filter-honesty` | medium | server | Compile only owning projects; add a severity-filtered diagnostics total |
| 7 | both | `server-guidance-callable-tools-autoreload` | medium | server | Name callable tools and the auto-reload default in server guidance |
| 8 | claude | `server-info-update-unknown-not-false` | medium (single-harness) | server | Report updateAvailable as unknown while the update check is pending |

### 4.1 `unknown-workspace-id-error-category`

- **Title:** Give an unknown workspaceId its own error, not the generic NotFound
- **Priority hint:** high. The failure reproduced in both harnesses across 3 sessions, deterministically, with the largest server-side call count (2a: 131 failed calls, 127 of them in claude b0eb94cb).
- **Surface:** server. 2a attributes it server-side in both harnesses. It is still unresolved whether b0eb94cb's Workflow subagents hit an evicted session or a separate server instance (2a), but either way the envelope comes from the server.
- **Summary:** In claude b0eb94cb the orchestrator told its scorers "workspace ALREADY LOADED, `workspaceId = "942a26bc7b3e4d24aa914cff9f377fb4"`" [Q1d]. Their calls returned "The requested item was not found. Ensure the workspace is loaded and the identifier is correct." with `"exceptionType": "KeyNotFoundException"` [Q1a], and codex child 01a06a3d got the same text from a cell that reloaded the workspace and then ran `mcp__roslyn__compile_check` [Q1b]. A wrong metadataName (codex child 01a06e2a [Q1c]) and a stale member symbolHandle (codex child 01a06a23, also `KeyNotFoundException` [Q1e]) return the identical message, so neither the text nor exceptionType tells a dead workspace apart from a bad identifier. Agents retried blind, reloaded, or fell back to grep (2a: 19 of 76 b0eb94cb scorers fell back to grep).
- **Proposed action:**
  - At WorkspaceManager.cs:1367, where the raw message already carries the id and the active-session count, throw a dedicated `WorkspaceNotFoundException : KeyNotFoundException`.
  - Register it in ToolErrorHandler ahead of the `KeyNotFoundException` entry (:119), the same way `WorkspaceEvictedException` is registered (:105-110). Give it its own category and a public message that states the active-session count and the `mcp__roslyn__workspace_list` → `mcp__roslyn__workspace_load` recovery.
  - `BuildSafeNotFoundMessage` (:559-572) then handles only symbol, document and handle misses.
  - A new category on a path that used to return `NotFound` changes the published wire contract, so ship it with a migration note.
- **Evidence:** 2a#multi-tool-workspace-id-notfound, 2a#find_references-symbolhandle-keynotfound, 2a#find_references-metadataname-symbolnotfound; 2b#workspace-already-loaded-dead-id (discoverability); 3#workspace-handle-lost-across-agent-boundary. Sessions: claude:b0eb94cb@dotnet-firewall-analyzer; codex:01a06a0e@dotnet-network-documentation (children 01a06a23, 01a06a3d), codex:01a06a0f@roslyn-backed-mcp, codex:01a06e26@bioremote (child 01a06e2a), codex:01a06417@tradewise.

### 4.2 `list-tool-response-byte-budget`

- **Title:** Keep oversized results retrievable in both clients; add a server byte budget
- **Priority hint:** high. This has the widest cross-harness spread in 2a: the Codex output cap hit 78 calls across 35 session tags and the Claude result cap hit 25 calls across 4 sessions, deterministically. Codex agents get partial data.
- **Surface:** codex-harness and claude-harness, matching 2a. 2a attributes both symptoms `harness-specific` (the Codex exec-output token cap and the Claude result wrapper), and the server payloads were well-formed, so this is not a server defect. The server byte budget in the proposed action is an optional enhancement that would help both clients; it does not change the attribution.
- **Summary:** Row limits do not bound bytes. Claude b0eb94cb called `mcp__roslyn__find_references` with `summary:true, limit:200` and got "Error: result (58,997 characters across 1,244 lines) exceeds maximum allowed tokens. Output has been saved to" [Q2a], and claude 9417cc0a's related-test sweep got "Error: result (147,962 characters across 1,937 lines) exceeds maximum allowed tokens." [Q2c]. Codex keeps the head of the output inline instead: `mcp__roslyn__find_references` at `limit:100` returned "Warning: truncated output (original token count: 12808)" (codex 01a06417 [Q2b]), and a cell batching `mcp__roslyn__get_source_text` and `mcp__roslyn__find_references` returned "Warning: truncated output (original token count: 10095)" (codex 01a055af [Q2d]). Claude fails loudly and saves the payload to disk; Codex returns a cut-off list that can look complete.
- **Proposed action:**
  - **Codex harness:** when exec output crosses the token cap, keep the full payload retrievable, as Claude does when it saves to disk ([Q2a]), instead of keeping only the inline head.
  - **Claude harness:** the cap already fails loudly and saves the payload, so no client change is proposed. The remaining cost is one extra read per oversized result.
  - **Server enhancement (optional, helps both clients):** add a serialized-size ceiling to the row-bounded list tools:
    - `mcp__roslyn__find_references`: `limit=100` (SymbolTools.cs:270)
    - `mcp__roslyn__test_related_files`: `maxResults=100` (ValidationTools.cs:468)
    - `mcp__roslyn__get_coupling_metrics`: `limit=100` (CouplingAnalysisTools.cs:24)
    - `mcp__roslyn__find_unused_symbols`: `limit=50` (AdvancedAnalysisTools.cs:24)
    - `mcp__roslyn__find_dead_fields`: `limit=50` (AdvancedAnalysisTools.cs:388)
  - Set the ceiling below the smallest truncated payload observed (10,095 tokens, [Q2d]). Stop adding rows once the JSON would cross it, and return `hasMore` plus the next `offset`. `mcp__roslyn__find_references` already accepts `offset` (SymbolTools.cs:271).
  - Start with `mcp__roslyn__find_references`, the only tool with a quoted truncation in both harnesses ([Q2a], [Q2b]).
- **Evidence:** 2a#multi-tool-codex-output-truncation, 2a#multi-tool-claude-result-size-cap; 2b#test_related_files-summary-groupBy (real gap), 2b#compact-tool-index (discoverability); 3#oversized-result-truncation-parity. ### 4.3 `compile-check-restore-required-gate`

- **Title:** Gate mcp__roslyn__compile_check on the load's restore/build-required flags
- **Agents:** claude. The phantom-error failure is quoted in claude only, which makes this single-harness. Codex is adjacent but has no phantom-error quote: it shows the flag still set after `autoRestore` [Q3e], and §3's scan finds codex in the same unrestored state in 32 sessions.
- **Priority hint:** high, single-harness (claude only). It is deterministic across 5 claude worktree sessions, and 2a records two executors that dropped Roslyn validation for the rest of their initiatives.
- **Surface:** server. 2a attributes it server-side: the server compiles a workspace that its own load reply flagged as not ready.
- **Summary:** Loading a fresh worktree reports `"restoreRequired": true,` (claude ca7969ce [Q3a]; claude 5051da13 [Q3b]) with a hint that sends agents to the shell ("Run `dotnet build` on the analyzer project, then `workspace_reload`.", claude 5051da13 [Q3b]). Yet `mcp__roslyn__compile_check` still runs, and in that same 5051da13 subagent it returned `"errorCount": 24654,` [Q3c]. In claude 9417cc0a the errors read "could not be found (are you missing a using directive or an assembly reference?)" next to `"restoreHint": null` [Q3d]; that follows from the tool's only restore signal, a heuristic that counts CS0234 errors. After `mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload{autoRestore:true}`, codex 01a06c9a still got "Package-restore inputs changed since the last restore. Run `dotnet restore` on the solution or project, then `workspace_reload`." [Q3e].
- **Proposed action:**
  - Have `mcp__roslyn__compile_check` read the session's `RestoreRequired` and `BuildRequired` flags. No `CompileCheck*.cs` file references either flag today. The only restore signal is the `cs0234Count >= 10` heuristic at CompileCheckService.cs:262-266, which ignores CS0246-shaped floods like [Q3d].
  - When either flag is set, return a structured not-ready result with no diagnostic list. It should name `mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload{autoRestore:true}` for the restore and `mcp__roslyn__build_project` for the analyzer project.
  - Change the hint at WorkspaceStatusSummaryDto.cs:161 to name `mcp__roslyn__build_project` instead of the CLI; 2b rates this a discoverability gap.
  - Separately, find out why `autoRestore` left `restoreRequired` set in [Q3e]; 2a leaves that attribution open.
- **Evidence:** 2a#compile_check-phantom-errors-unrestored-workspace, 2a#workspace_load-not-ready-shell-restore-hint, 2a#workspace_load-restorerequired-persists-after-reload; 2b#worktree-isReady-build_project (discoverability); 3#unrestored-worktree-phantom-diagnostics. ### 4.4 `codex-exec-cell-mcp-await-cutoff`

- **Title:** Keep Codex exec cells yielding while only MCP calls are outstanding
- **Priority hint:** high on volume, but single-harness (codex only). It is deterministic across 54 session tags with 174 empty cells (2a), and each cutoff costs at least one `wait` round trip.
- **Surface:** codex-harness. 2a attributes it harness-specific: the text is Codex's exec wall-clock message, with no server envelope. This is not a server defect.
- **Summary:** A Codex exec cell still waiting on `mcp__roslyn__test_run` at about 31 s returns `Script running with cell ID 63\nWall time 31.0 seconds\nOutput:` (codex 01a043bb [Q4a]) or `Script running with cell ID 47\nWall time 31.1 seconds` (codex 01a06e27 [Q4b]), and the model has to poll for a result the server is still producing. Claude has no per-cell clock. The same tool runs until 120 s, when Claude reports it "is still running after 120s. It was moved to the background as task k21d3sus7" (claude a3c84938 [Q4c]); the agent then wrote "The full-suite run hit the server's 120s cap (expected for this large suite — CI runs it sharded)." [Q4d]. Server phase budgets can also expire under the cutoff: "validation phase 'project_diagnostics' exceeded the internal timeout of 25 second(s); retryable=true" (codex 01a0498c [Q4e]).
- **Proposed action:**
  - In the Codex exec harness: when a cell's only pending awaits are MCP tool calls, keep the cell yielding until they finish, bounded by the MCP call's own timeout, instead of returning `Script running` at 31 s.
  - On the server side, the complement is 2b's proposed `mcp__roslyn__operation_start`/`mcp__roslyn__operation_status` handle for `mcp__roslyn__test_run`, `mcp__roslyn__project_diagnostics`, `mcp__roslyn__validate_*` and `mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload` (real gap, recurring, cross-harness). It would let either client reattach to a running call.
  - The live row `test-run-full-suite-timeout-envelope` does not provide reattachment.
- **Evidence:** 2a#multi-tool-codex-exec-31s-cutoff, 2a#test_run-claude-120s-background-move (parity), 2a#validate_recent_git_changes-internal-25s-timeout; 2b#operation_start-operation_status (real, recurring, cross-harness); 3#codex-exec-31s-cutoff-forces-poll. ### 4.5 `preview-refusal-public-reasons`

- **Title:** Replace generic InvalidOperation/InvalidArgument text with reviewed reasons
- **Priority hint:** medium. Preview refusals span 6 tools in 7 codex sessions (§3 scan: 12 envelopes). Generic argument text is deterministic in 8 codex sessions and 1 claude session (2a), so both harnesses hit it. Every refusal ended in hand edits, but no single failure mode appears in more than 4 sessions.
- **Surface:** server. 2a attributes every contributing row server-side, and the text comes from ToolErrorHandler.
- **Summary:** Previews refuse with "The operation is not valid in the current state. Check the tool contract and retry." (codex 01a054a4, `mcp__roslyn__move_type_to_file_preview` [Q5a]), even when the server knows the cause: the `mcp__roslyn__extract_type_preview` envelope in codex child 01a06ec1 carries `ExtractTypeBlockingDependencyException` behind that same sentence [Q5b]. Argument errors are just as vague:
  - "Parameter 'edits' is invalid. Check that all required parameters are provided and values match the expected types." (codex 01a04fb3 [Q5c])
  - "Parameter 'arguments' is invalid." for an unknown `solutionPath` key (codex child 01a06a49 [Q5d])
  - `Parameter '<unknown>' is invalid` for a loose `.cs` path, and "Parameter 'path' is invalid." for a `.csproj` outside the sanctioned roots (claude 9408fc2e [Q5e], [Q5f])

  With nothing to correct, agents dropped the tool: "Its extract-method preview could not represent the stateful region safely (`InvalidOperation`), so I’m applying the explicit fallback" (codex 01a05db7 [Q5g]).
- **Proposed action:**
  - Keep the redaction-safe fallbacks (ToolErrorHandler.cs:173-176 and :645), but stop sending known refusals through them. Throw `PublicInvalidOperationException` or `PublicArgumentException` with reviewed reasons that don't echo caller input; both already bypass the fallback (ToolErrorHandler.cs:159-161 and :579-582).
  - Start with three sites:
    1. TypeMoveService, which throws 6 plain `InvalidOperationException`s (e.g. "target file exists", "type not found"). `mcp__roslyn__move_type_to_file_preview` produced 6 of the 12 envelopes.
    2. EditService.cs:579-584, the only `nameof(edits)` throw in src. It builds a message with both edit indices and their ranges, which the generic fallback then discards. Keep the indices and ranges; drop only the file path.
    3. StructuredCallToolFilter, which already inspects raw arguments before the SDK binder (:128-130). Name the unknown key and the missing required parameter instead of `'arguments'`.
  - Today `PublicInvalidOperationException` is thrown in only 4 files (11 sites), none of them TypeMoveService, TypeExtractionService or ExtractMethodService.
- **Evidence:** 2a#move_type_to_file_preview-generic-invalidoperation, 2a#extract_type_preview-blocking-dependency-generic-message, 2a#extract_method_preview-invalidoperation-stateful-region, 2a#change_type_namespace_preview-generic-invalidoperation, 2a#symbol_refactor_preview-restructure-invalidoperation, 2a#preview_multi_file_edit-invalidoperation, 2a#preview_multi_file_edit-invalidargument-edits, 2a#multi-tool-unknown-parameter-name-invalidargument, 2a#workspace_load-invalidargument-hides-path-reason; 2b#preview_patch_edit (real gap); 3#opaque-preview-refusal-forces-apply-patch. ### 4.6 `diagnostics-scope-and-filter-honesty`

- **Title:** Compile only owning projects; add a severity-filtered diagnostics total
- **Priority hint:** medium. It covers two of the four modes that 2a reproduced in both harnesses: file-scope widening (5 sessions) and severity totals (3 sessions), both deterministic. The cost is wall time and misread results, not failed calls.
- **Surface:** server. 2a attributes both server-side: one is server scope resolution, the other the server envelope.
- **Summary:** An `mcp__roslyn__compile_check` over one prod file and one test file reported "compile_check file filter fallback: supplied file scope resolved to multiple projects; compiled the full project scope and filtered returned diagnostics by file path." with `"requestedScope": "files"` and `"actualScope": "solution"` (claude 1bec7d73 [Q6a]); codex child 01a06d54 hit the same fallback [Q6b]. `mcp__roslyn__project_diagnostics` with `severity:"Warning"` returned `"totalInfo": 2932,` beside `"distinctDiagnosticIds": 0,` and `"diagnosticGroups": []` (claude 9c0caec1 [Q6c]), and in codex 01a05439 it returned `"totalDiagnostics": 29,` beside `"returnedDiagnostics": 0,` [Q6d]. At HEAD, the widening is reported in the structured `actualScope` field, and the `total*` fields ignore the severity filter by design (DiagnosticsResultDto.cs:11), but neither the tool description (AnalysisTools.cs:22) nor the `severity` parameter description (:31) says so. By contrast, `mcp__roslyn__compile_check`'s `totalDiagnostics` field counts only filter matches (CompileCheckDto.cs:11).
- **Proposed action:**
  - (a) When a file scope resolves to more than one project, compile only those projects. CompileCheckService.cs:330-352 already computes `owningProjects` but returns the full `filteredProjects` list, and the comment at :349-350 wrongly calls that widening correct. Report the narrower scope through the existing `actualScope` field; its vocabulary is tracked by the live row `compile-check-scope-vocab-and-untested-branches`.
  - (b) Add a new field to `mcp__roslyn__project_diagnostics`, next to the unfiltered `total*` fields (AnalysisTools.cs:93, :108), that counts only diagnostics matching `severity`/`diagnosticId`. Also say in the `severity` description (:31) that the `total*` fields ignore the filter.
- **Evidence:** 2a#compile_check-file-scope-widened-to-solution, 2a#project_diagnostics-severity-filter-skips-totals; 2b: none; 3: none (not a §3 pattern). ### 4.7 `server-guidance-callable-tools-autoreload`

- **Title:** Name callable tools and the auto-reload default in server guidance
- **Priority hint:** medium. §3's scan finds both habits in both harnesses:

  | agent | habit | sessions (§3 scan) |
  |---|---|---|
  | codex | explicit reloads | 117 |
  | claude | explicit reloads | 4 |
  | codex | tool-discovery lookups | 196 |
  | claude | tool-discovery lookups | 23 |

  However, the router dead end is quoted only in codex (2a: 5 sessions, deterministic), and all the reload quotes are codex.
- **Surface:** server. This is guidance text plus the router payload the server ships (2a attributes `mcp__roslyn__recommend_workflow` server-side). No behavior change is involved.
- **Summary:** `mcp__roslyn__recommend_workflow`'s no-match branch told codex 01a05ef7 "The task does not match a high-confidence first-hop rule; ask for a category-specific capability view before selecting tools." and returned `\\\"primaryTools\\\": [\\r\\n    \\\"discover_capabilities\\\"\\r\\n  ]` [Q7a], but `discover_capabilities` is registered as a prompt (RoslynPrompts.AnalysisWorkflows.cs:90), not a tool; codex 01a06417 got the same fallback [Q7b]. C# token searches went to text tools, `grep -rn "ValidationFilter<" --include=*.cs src | wc -l` (claude b0eb94cb [Q7c]) and `rg -n -F "UnexpectedExceptionReporting.Report" src tests` (codex 01a05952 [Q7d]), even though `mcp__roslyn__semantic_grep` covers this. The bootstrap's navigation line lists categories, not that tool, and 2b found no session that looked it up. Codex also reloads before its checks, as in `const reload=await tools.mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload({workspaceId:ws,autoRestore:false,verbose:false});` (codex 01a053cf [Q7e]), and each reload holds the gate (`"heldMs": 10345`, codex 01a048f7 [Q7f]), even though the server already reloads stale workspaces on its own and stamps `"staleAction": "auto-reloaded",` (codex 01a05d28 [Q7g]).
- **Proposed action:**
  1. In WorkflowRecommendationTools.cs:105-110, replace `discover_capabilities` with callable tools per category, e.g. `mcp__roslyn__symbol_search`, `mcp__roslyn__find_references`, `mcp__roslyn__semantic_grep`, `mcp__roslyn__get_source_text`, `mcp__roslyn__compile_check`. The payload itself uses bare names.
  2. In ServerInstructions.cs, name `mcp__roslyn__semantic_grep` on the navigation line of the experimental-profile `Text` (:15). It is experimental-tier, so leave `StableOnlyText` unchanged.
  3. In the same `Text`, add one sentence: workspace-scoped calls auto-reload a stale workspace that the file watcher has flagged (default `OnStale = StalenessPolicy.AutoReload`, ExecutionGateOptions.cs:59) and report it in `_meta.staleAction`. An explicit `mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload` is then needed only when a response shows `staleAction: "warn"` or `restoreRequired: true`, not before every check. `Text` is about 1,150 of the 2,048-character `ClientCharacterLimit`.
  4. Put the same default in the `mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload` description (WorkspaceTools.cs:103), which today presents reloading as the way to pick up file changes.
- **Evidence:** 2a#recommend_workflow-generic-discover-capabilities-fallback, 2a#workspace_reload-gate-hold-latency, 2a#apply_composite_preview-token-stale-after-branch-switch; 2b#token-search-semantic_grep (discoverability, recurring, cross-harness), 2b#compile_check-autoReload (real gap), 2b#compact-tool-index (discoverability); 3#per-agent-tool-discovery-tax, 3#defensive-reload-after-out-of-band-edit. Sessions: codex:01a05ef7@dotnet-firewall-analyzer, codex:01a06417@tradewise, codex:01a05d28@tradewise, codex:01a06396@it-chat-bot, codex:01a03ecb@dotnet-network-documentation, codex:01a05952@roslyn-backed-mcp, codex:01a053cf@dotnet-firewall-analyzer, codex:01a048f7@tradewise, codex:01a03fee@dotnet-firewall-analyzer; claude:b0eb94cb@dotnet-firewall-analyzer.

### 4.8 `server-info-update-unknown-not-false`

- **Title:** Report updateAvailable as unknown while the update check is pending
- **Priority hint:** medium. It is deterministic in 5 claude release and version-check sessions (all 10 probes, 2a). By quote it is single-harness: §3's scan shows codex receiving the same `pending` block in 83 sessions, with no quoted impact. The cost lands in release cuts, where 2a records one session treating the value as authoritative.
- **Surface:** server. 2a attributes the pending-update row server-side: the update block is server JSON, identical for every client. The adjacent stale-running-version symptom is not part of this finding; 2a attributes it `unknown` and routes it to docs/process, so it is listed below the cut.
- **Summary:** `mcp__roslyn__server_info` probes returned `"update":{"current":"4.1.1","latest":null,"updateAvailable":false,"command":null,"checkStatus":"pending","lastCheckedAt":null}` (claude 532d14a3 [Q8a]), and the same block at 4.1.0 (claude cb593a23 [Q8b]) and 4.0.0 (claude 3c572d4b [Q8c]). In each case `false` meant "not checked yet", not "up to date". Release agents then queried the feed by hand, `curl -s "https://api.nuget.org/v3/registration5-gz-semver2/darylmcd.roslynmcp/index.json" --compressed` (claude 532d14a3 [Q8d]), and one live-version check got "Version 4.1.1 of package darylmcd.roslynmcp is not found in NuGet feeds https://api.nuget.org/v3/index.json." (claude b4a3a083 [Q8e]).
- **Proposed action:**
  - At ServerTools.cs:281-299, emit `updateAvailable: null` unless the check status is succeeded.
  - Today `updateAvailable = latestVersion is not null && …` turns pending into `false`. The first probe of every process is pending because `GetLatestVersion` starts the fetch only on first access (NuGetVersionChecker.cs:195-199).
  - `ServerUpdateInfoDto.UpdateAvailable` is a non-nullable `bool` (ServerToolDtos.cs:136), so this changes the published wire shape and needs a migration note.
  - Forcing a check is the follow-on 2b gap, the proposed `mcp__roslyn__server_update_check{force}`. The live row `nuget-version-checker-fetch-decomposition` is a quality refactor and covers neither.
- **Evidence:** 2a#server_info-update-check-pending-reports-no-update; 2b#server_update_check (real gap), 2b#server_install_status (real gap, recurring); 3#install-layer-version-drift. Sessions: claude:3c572d4b@roslyn-backed-mcp, claude:532d14a3@roslyn-backed-mcp, claude:cb593a23@roslyn-backed-mcp, claude:b4a3a083@roslyn-backed-mcp, claude:ee6273ee@roslyn-backed-mcp.
