## 5. Meta-note

**Counting basis.** All call counts in this section come from 2a: calls in the 187 included sessions, grouped by session tag, after the window correction. Figures marked §3 are §3's own counts. They appear only where 2a gives no number. Rankings below compare 2a figures only. §0 and §3 report the same client-limit problems in other units, and their numbers do not match 2a:

| phenomenon | §0 | 2a (used here) | §3 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Codex 31 s cutoff | 257 Roslyn-bearing cells | 174 calls, 54 session tags | 155 cells, 76 sessions |
| Codex output truncation | 450 | 78 calls, 35 session tags | 243 cells, 100 sessions |
| Claude result cap | 24 | 25 calls | 36, in the same 4 sessions |
| Stale-workspace NotFound in b0eb94cb | — | 127 | 130 |

- For the Codex rows, the different units (cells vs calls, session tags vs sessions) may explain the gaps. This report does not show that they do.
- For the Claude result cap, units cannot explain the gap: the same 4 sessions give both 25 and 36. The count is wrong somewhere and still needs to be fixed. Nothing in this section depends on which figure is right.

**Dropped sessions per source** (`stats.dropped_by_source`, same figures as §0): claude 363, codex 131, both after the window correction. Separately, 2 in-window Codex sessions were never extracted (01a03e20@dotnet-firewall-analyzer, 01a03e21@dotnet-network-documentation; §0). They count as neither dropped nor included.

**Phase mix per harness**

| agent | refactoring | release-operational | planning-docs | mixed | sessions |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| claude | 0 | 4 | 1 | 12 | 17 |
| codex | 69 | 10 | 11 | 80 | 170 |
| both | 69 | 14 | 12 | 92 | 187 |

- **Sample balance.**
  - Claude is 9% of included sessions after the correction (17 of 187; 15% before). That is under the 20% line, so this report mostly describes Codex.
  - Most Claude evidence comes from one session. b0eb94cb holds 478 of 672 Claude calls (71%) and 127 of the 131 stale-workspace NotFound failures (2a).
  - 22 of 30 Claude-only 2a modes rest on a single session, against 17 of 30 Codex-only modes. Read Claude-only rows as proof that a failure mode exists, not as a measure of how often it happens.
- **Where friction concentrates.** The two harnesses differ.
  - **Codex:** mostly the ergonomics of its exec bridge. That means the 31 s cutoff (174 calls across 54 session tags, 2a), the output cap (78 calls across 35 session tags, 2a) and repeated catalog lookups. Second is the reliability of preview/apply refactorings: 9 modes, 7 of them refusals that give no reason.
  - **Claude:** mostly whether workspaces are ready and their ids stay valid. That means phantom compile errors on unrestored worktrees in 5 sessions, and stale workspace ids across Workflow subagents. Analyzer false positives and version reporting in release sessions add to it.
  - **Coverage:** 2b lists 29 real gaps, but only 4 recur and 3 appear in both harnesses.
  - **Docs and guidance:** the recommend_workflow fallback and the undocumented auto-reload default affect both harnesses.
- **Server-side vs harness-specific.**
  - By failure mode: 51 server-side, 8 harness-specific, 5 unknown.
  - By affected calls (2a), client limits lead. The Codex cutoff has 174 calls and Codex truncation 78, plus 25 Claude result-cap diversions. The largest server mode has 131 calls.
  - Server fixes should start with the 4 modes seen in both harnesses and with response size. A byte budget would also reduce truncation in both clients. The Codex exec cutoff needs a fix in the Codex harness; it is not a server defect.
- **Repo skew.**
  - **Specific to scale or one repo:**
    - TradeWise's 13-project solution causes the validation timeouts and has the most cutoff session tags (16 of 54, 2a).
    - b0eb94cb's Workflow fan-out in dotnet-firewall-analyzer causes the NotFound volume.
    - Maintainer release sessions in roslyn-backed-mcp supply every `mcp__roslyn__server_info` update-check row.
  - **Not tied to one repo:** wrong severity totals, file-scope widening, and `.sln`/`.slnx` FileNotFound each appear in 2–5 sessions across both harnesses. Refusals that give no reason span 3 repos.
- **One default-usage change per harness.**
  - **codex:** after an `apply_patch` edit, call `mcp__roslyn__compile_check` directly instead of pairing it with `mcp__roslyn__workspace_reload`. Reload only when a response shows `staleAction: warn` or `restoreRequired: true`. Per §3's figures, the server already auto-reloaded stale workspaces 187 times in 46 sessions. Meanwhile 178 reload+check cells in 69 sessions spent part of the 31 s cell budget waiting for the reload.
  - **claude:** every spawned subagent, Workflow runs included, should call `mcp__roslyn__workspace_load` with the solution path (loading the same path twice is safe). It should not trust a workspaceId passed in prompt text. That id was already dead for b0eb94cb's Workflow scorers 127 times (2a).
- **Window length.**
  - **Codex:** 14 days is enough. There are 170 sessions, and the top modes recur across 35–54 session tags (2a).
  - **Claude:** 14 days is too short. There are 17 sessions, one holds 71% of calls, and most Claude-only modes rest on a single session. Next time, widen the Claude side (for example to 28 days) or rebalance.
  - **Spec change:** select sessions by record timestamps (first record ≤ window end, last record ≥ window start), not by file mtime. Mtime failed on both harnesses:
    - Claude bulk rewrites pulled 15 pre-window sessions into the mtime window.
    - Codex mtime stays at file creation, which hid 2 in-window sessions.
    - The 4 post-window Codex sessions came in because the extractor had no end bound.
